LT EN

Finite computational checks

Inspect and run the computations cited in the lecture notes. The downloadable Python files retain their original checks and deliberately wrong-answer controls.

Download the released checkers (ZIP) Reading guide

The code, comments and program output are in English. The same mathematical conventions apply to both language editions.

What the output means

EXACT uses exact arithmetic. SYMBOLIC checks an algebraic identity. NUMERICAL evaluates selected instances with floating-point tolerances. The claim beside each result states what was checked.

A control deliberately tries a wrong formula. It passes when that wrong formula is rejected. Read the surrounding claim before interpreting an OK line.

A finite check supports the worked computation. It does not replace a general proof, its hypotheses, a cited classification or construction, or a physical principle. These scripts also do not verify the geometry of the diagrams.

Run a computation

Extract the ZIP and open its category-theory-checks folder in a terminal. Read README.md and the selected source file. Use a Python environment with the package versions recorded in requirements.txt: NumPy 1.26.4, SymPy 1.14.0 and mpmath 1.3.0. The companion was tested with Python 3.11.14. The runner installs nothing.

List the released files, then run a lecture by its number:

python3 run_checks.py --list
python3 run_checks.py 7
python3 run_checks.py --independent

The explicit --independent selection runs all released checkers that do not need the separate Lecture 8 package. Some symbolic calculations take longer than a minute. A failed check returns a nonzero exit status.

Lecture 8 needs one additional package

The Lecture 8 checker compares its classical limit with rep-engine 0.0.3. This package is not bundled. The build used a local source checkout; availability from a public package index is not assumed. Its exact version requirement is recorded in requirements-l8.txt. If you have that package installed, select 8 or --all. Without it, selecting Lecture 8 fails with an explanation; its comparison is never silently skipped. Sage and the optional gmpy2 speedup are not needed.

What is included

The ZIP contains the original check-lN-algebra.py files for released English lectures and their required local Python helpers. It excludes private lecture sources, source-locking self-study checks, exercise sheets, review material and build tools. The README gives the scope and dependency provenance. A manifest records the file hashes and release selection.

Read the released source

Lecture 1: check-l1-algebra.py 
"""Algebra script for Lecture 1, *Categories: a theory of systems and processes*.

Every formula, number and diagram equation that lectures/lec01.tex prints is checked here
before it is printed, labelled SYMBOLIC (sympy, generic entries), EXACT (integers, exact
permutation matrices), NUMERICAL (floating point on fixed or random instances).  A diagram
equation is checked as an equation between composites of matrices, read with time running
upward (Bible K1): a chain of boxes f, then g, then h, from the bottom, is h @ g @ f.  The
three diagram equations of L1 are exact equalities, with no scalar.

The running example is S3 acting on C^3 by permuting coordinates (Symmetries L1, L3):
rho(s) e_i = e_{s(i)}.  Permutations compose as functions, (s t)(i) = s(t(i)), so t acts
first; as strands (Symmetries lec01.tex:541-550) t is the lower layer.

Labels are the lecture's \\label{} keys (a parenthesis names the item within it).  A check
tests a printed claim on instances: exhaustively over S3, symbolically for generic entries
of fixed shapes, or on random samples.  It does not prove a general quantifier (the
lecture's proofs do that), and it does not parse the TeX.  Most checks carry a control that
must fail, so that a check which cannot fail is visible.  Not checked here: the citations
(checked against the PDFs) and the two pictures without equations (the braid and the pair
of pants), apart from the permutation the braid is said to induce.

Run:  python3 scripts/check-l1-algebra.py     (exit status 1 on failure)
"""
import itertools
import sys

import numpy as np
import sympy as sp

FAILS = []
RNG = np.random.default_rng(20261021)


def check(label, kind, ok, what):
    print(f"{'OK  ' if ok else 'FAIL'} [{kind:9s}] {label}: {what}")
    if not ok:
        FAILS.append(label)


def control(label, kind, failed, what):
    """A must-fail control: `failed` is True when the wrong statement is indeed refuted."""
    check(label + ' (control)', kind, failed, what + ' -- must fail, and does')


def crandn(*shape):
    return RNG.standard_normal(shape) + 1j * RNG.standard_normal(shape)


def close(a, b, tol=1e-10):
    return np.allclose(a, b, atol=tol, rtol=0)


# ================================================================ S3 and its representation
S3 = list(itertools.permutations(range(3)))           # s as the tuple (s(0), s(1), s(2))
ONE = (0, 1, 2)


def compose(s, t):
    """s o t: t first, then s."""
    return tuple(s[t[i]] for i in range(3))


def inverse(s):
    r = [0] * 3
    for i, si in enumerate(s):
        r[si] = i
    return tuple(r)


def rho(s):
    """rho(s) e_i = e_{s(i)}: the permutation matrix with column i equal to e_{s(i)}."""
    M = sp.zeros(3, 3)
    for i in range(3):
        M[s[i], i] = 1
    return M


def transposition(i, j):
    t = list(range(3))
    t[i], t[j] = t[j], t[i]
    return tuple(t)


# ===================================================================== S1.2 categories
# eq:1:chain -- a chain of boxes f, g, h (time upward) is one morphism: h(gf) = (hg)f.
f, g, h = crandn(3, 2), crandn(4, 3), crandn(2, 4)
check('eq:1:chain', 'NUMERICAL', close(h @ (g @ f), (h @ g) @ f),
      'the chain f, g, h from the bottom: h o (g o f) = (h o g) o f, as matrices 2x2')
check('eq:1:chain(units)', 'NUMERICAL', close(np.eye(3) @ f, f) and close(f @ np.eye(2), f),
      'an identity box may be erased: id o f = f = f o id')
fs, gs, hs = crandn(2, 2), crandn(2, 2), crandn(2, 2)
control('eq:1:chain(order)', 'NUMERICAL', not close(hs @ gs @ fs, fs @ gs @ hs),
        'reading the chain downward (f o g o h instead of h o g o f, square matrices)')

# ex:1:categories(BG) -- the group axioms of S3 are the category axioms of BS3.
assoc = all(compose(a, compose(b, c)) == compose(compose(a, b), c)
            for a in S3 for b in S3 for c in S3)
units = all(compose(a, ONE) == a == compose(ONE, a) for a in S3)
check('ex:1:categories(BG)', 'EXACT', assoc and units,
      'BS3: composition g o h = gh is associative with identity 1 (all 6^3 triples)')
check('def:1:iso(BG)', 'EXACT', all(compose(a, inverse(a)) == ONE == compose(inverse(a), a) for a in S3),
      'BS3 is a groupoid: every g has the inverse g^{-1}')

# ex:1:categories(Rep) -- intertwiners compose.
a_, b_, c_, d_ = sp.symbols('a b c d')
J3 = sp.ones(3, 3)
xi_gen = a_ * sp.eye(3) + b_ * J3                      # the generic intertwiner C^3 -> C^3
eta_gen = c_ * sp.eye(3) + d_ * J3
ok = all(sp.simplify(xi_gen * rho(s) - rho(s) * xi_gen) == sp.zeros(3, 3) for s in S3)
ok2 = all(sp.simplify(eta_gen * xi_gen * rho(s) - rho(s) * eta_gen * xi_gen) == sp.zeros(3, 3)
          for s in S3)
check('ex:1:categories(Rep)', 'SYMBOLIC', ok and ok2,
      'a I + b J intertwines rho with itself, and so does the composite of two such')

# ex:1:categories(braid) and ex:1:functors(braid) -- the drawn braid: a crossing of strands
# 1, 2 at the bottom, then of strands 2, 3; the strand starting at i ends at pi(i).
tau1, tau2 = transposition(0, 1), transposition(1, 2)
pi = compose(tau2, tau1)                               # tau1 first (lower layer)
check('ex:1:functors(braid)', 'EXACT', pi == (2, 0, 1),
      'the braid induces 1 -> 3, 2 -> 1, 3 -> 2, the permutation tau2 tau1 = (1 3 2)')
control('ex:1:functors(braid order)', 'EXACT', compose(tau1, tau2) != (2, 0, 1),
        'stacking in the other order, tau1 tau2, gives a different permutation')

# def:1:iso -- the inverse is unique: if g and h are both two-sided inverses of f, then
# h = h o (f o g) = (h o f) o g = g.  Here g is computed as a right inverse (solve f g = I)
# and h as a left inverse (solve h f = I, transposed), independently, and every step of
# the printed chain is compared.
fi = crandn(3, 3)
gi = np.linalg.solve(fi, np.eye(3))                    # f g = I
hi = np.linalg.solve(fi.T, np.eye(3)).T                # h f = I
steps = [hi, hi @ (fi @ gi), (hi @ fi) @ gi, gi]
check('def:1:iso(unique)', 'NUMERICAL', all(close(x, y) for x, y in zip(steps, steps[1:])),
      'h = h o (f o g) = (h o f) o g = g, each step')
fd = np.diag([2.0, 3.0, 5.0])
bad_steps = [np.eye(3), np.eye(3) @ (fd @ np.eye(3)), (np.eye(3) @ fd) @ np.eye(3), np.eye(3)]
control('def:1:iso(unique)', 'NUMERICAL', not all(close(x, y) for x, y in zip(bad_steps, bad_steps[1:])),
        'the chain with g = h = id for f = diag(2, 3, 5), which is not their inverse')

# ======================================================================= S1.3 functors
# ex:1:functors(dual) -- (g o f)* = f* o g*: in dual bases the dual map is the transpose
# (Linear Algebra prop:dualtranspose), so the claim is (GF)^T = F^T G^T.
F_ = sp.Matrix(3, 2, sp.symbols('f0:6'))
G_ = sp.Matrix(2, 3, sp.symbols('g0:6'))
check('ex:1:functors(dual)', 'SYMBOLIC', sp.simplify((G_ * F_).T - F_.T * G_.T) == sp.zeros(2, 2),
      '(g o f)* = f* o g*: the dual reverses composition (contravariant)')
Fq = sp.Matrix(2, 2, sp.symbols('m0:4'))
Gq = sp.Matrix(2, 2, sp.symbols('n0:4'))
control('ex:1:functors(dual covariant)', 'SYMBOLIC',
        sp.simplify((Gq * Fq).T - Gq.T * Fq.T) != sp.zeros(2, 2),
        '(g o f)* = g* o f* for square f, g (where both sides are defined)')
# the dual as a functor on functionals, without bases: f*(phi) = phi o f
phi = crandn(1, 2)
fv, gv = crandn(2, 3), crandn(2, 2)                    # f: C^3 -> C^2, g: C^2 -> C^2
star = lambda m: (lambda p: p @ m)                     # noqa: E731  f*(phi) = phi o f
check('ex:1:functors(dual, functionals)', 'NUMERICAL', close(star(gv @ fv)(phi), star(fv)(star(gv)(phi))),
      '(g o f)*(phi) = phi o g o f = f*(g*(phi)) on a sample')

# lem:1:iso -- functors preserve isomorphisms: F(f^{-1}) = F(f)^{-1}; for BG -> Vect_fd,
# every rho(g) is invertible with rho(g^{-1}) = rho(g)^{-1}.
check('lem:1:iso', 'EXACT', all(rho(inverse(s)) * rho(s) == sp.eye(3) for s in S3),
      'rho(g^{-1}) rho(g) = id for every g in S3')

# =========================================== S1.4 natural transformations; Rep = Fun(BG, Vect)
# thm:1:repfun -- the functor axioms are the homomorphism axioms.
check('thm:1:repfun(functor)', 'EXACT',
      all(rho(compose(s, t)) == rho(s) * rho(t) for s in S3 for t in S3) and rho(ONE) == sp.eye(3),
      'rho(gh) = rho(g) rho(h) and rho(1) = id: rho is a functor BS3 -> Vect_fd')
wrong = lambda s: rho(s).T                             # noqa: E731  the transpose reverses order
control('thm:1:repfun(functor)', 'EXACT',
        not all(wrong(compose(s, t)) == wrong(s) * wrong(t) for s in S3 for t in S3),
        's -> rho(s)^T as a covariant functor')

# ex:1:s3 -- the sum s: C^3 -> C (trivial representation) is natural; p_1 is not.
s_map = sp.Matrix([[1, 1, 1]])
p1 = sp.Matrix([[1, 0, 0]])
u_map = sp.Matrix([1, 1, 1])
triv = lambda s: sp.Matrix([[1]])                      # noqa: E731
check('ex:1:s3(sum)', 'EXACT', all(triv(t) * s_map == s_map * rho(t) for t in S3),
      's(rho(g) x) = s(x): the sum is a natural transformation rho => trivial')
check('ex:1:s3(u)', 'EXACT', all(rho(t) * u_map == u_map * triv(t) for t in S3),
      'u(1) = e1 + e2 + e3 is natural trivial => rho')
check('ex:1:s3(composite)', 'EXACT', u_map * s_map == J3 and all(J3 * rho(t) == rho(t) * J3 for t in S3),
      'u o s = J (every entry 1), and J commutes with every permutation matrix')
t12 = transposition(0, 1)
e1 = sp.Matrix([1, 0, 0])
check('ex:1:s3(p1)', 'EXACT', (p1 * rho(t12) * e1)[0, 0] == 0 and (p1 * e1)[0, 0] == 1,
      'p1(rho((1 2)) e1) = p1(e2) = 0, but p1(e1) = 1: p1 is not natural')

# eq:1:naturality and eq:1:vertical -- the sliding pictures with three different functors
# BS3 -> Vect_fd: F = rho on C^3, F' = the trivial representation on C, F'' = P rho P^{-1} on
# C^3 for a fixed non-monomial P, so F'' != F (C.3-L01 round 2); xi = s : F => F' (the sum)
# and zeta = P u : F' => F''.  For every g in S3,
#   F(g), xi, zeta (bottom to top)  =  xi, F'(g), zeta  =  xi, zeta, F''(g).
Pm = sp.Matrix([[1, 1, 0], [0, 1, 1], [1, 0, 2]])
rho2 = lambda t: Pm * rho(t) * Pm.inv()              # noqa: E731  F''
zeta = Pm * u_map
check('eq:1:naturality', 'EXACT', all(triv(t) * s_map == s_map * rho(t) for t in S3)
      and all(rho2(t) * zeta == zeta * triv(t) for t in S3),
      "F'(g) o xi = xi o F(g) for xi = s, and F''(g) o zeta = zeta o F'(g) for zeta = P u")
check("eq:1:naturality(F'' != F)", 'EXACT', any(rho2(t) != rho(t) for t in S3),
      "F'' = P rho P^{-1} is a different functor from F")
ok = True
for t in S3:
    left = zeta * s_map * rho(t)                       # F(g), then xi, then zeta
    mid = zeta * triv(t) * s_map                       # xi, then F'(g), then zeta
    right = rho2(t) * zeta * s_map                     # xi, zeta, then F''(g)
    ok &= left == mid == right
check('eq:1:vertical', 'EXACT', ok, "the two slides: zeta xi F(g) = zeta F'(g) xi = F''(g) zeta xi, all g in S3")
control('eq:1:vertical(top box)', 'EXACT',
        any(rho(t) * zeta * s_map != zeta * s_map * rho(t) for t in S3),
        "with F(g) instead of F''(g) in the top box of the right-hand picture, the pictures agree")
check('eq:1:vertical(composite)', 'EXACT', all(rho2(t) * zeta * s_map == zeta * s_map * rho(t) for t in S3),
      "the composite zeta o xi = P u s is natural from F to F''")
bad = sp.diag(1, 2, 3)
control('eq:1:vertical', 'EXACT',
        any(zeta * s_map * bad * rho(t) != rho2(t) * zeta * s_map * bad for t in S3),
        'with xi replaced by s o diag(1, 2, 3), not natural, the outer pictures agree')

# ======================================================== S1.5 equivalence; V** vs V*
# thm:1:doubledual(a) -- a_V(v)(phi) = phi(v) is natural: f**(a_V(v)) = a_W(f(v)).
n, m = 3, 2
fm = crandn(m, n)                                      # f: V = C^3 -> W = C^2
v = crandn(n, 1)
a_V = lambda vec: (lambda functional: (functional @ vec)[0, 0])   # noqa: E731
fstar = lambda psi: psi @ fm                           # noqa: E731  f*: W* -> V*
fss = lambda x: (lambda psi: x(fstar(psi)))           # noqa: E731  f**(x) = x o f*
ok = True
for _ in range(5):
    psi = crandn(1, m)
    ok &= abs(fss(a_V(v))(psi) - a_V(fm @ v)(psi)) < 1e-10
check('thm:1:doubledual(a)', 'NUMERICAL', ok,
      'f**(a_V(v))(psi) = psi(f(v)) = a_W(f(v))(psi) on samples')

# thm:1:doubledual(b) -- for f = i id_V, f* theta f = i^2 theta = -theta for every linear theta.
k = 3
Theta = sp.Matrix(k, k, sp.symbols('t0:9'))            # the matrix of theta: V -> V* in dual bases
fI = sp.I * sp.eye(k)
check('thm:1:doubledual(b)', 'SYMBOLIC', sp.simplify(fI.T * Theta * fI + Theta) == sp.zeros(k, k),
      'f = i id: f* o theta o f = -theta, so naturality forces theta = 0')
control('thm:1:doubledual(b) with f = -id', 'SYMBOLIC',
        sp.simplify((-sp.eye(k)).T * Theta * (-sp.eye(k)) + Theta) != sp.zeros(k, k),
        'f = -id refutes nothing (f* theta f = theta), which is why the proof takes i')
# the same with the naturality square written as theta_W o f = (f*)^{-1} o theta_V
check('thm:1:doubledual(b, square)', 'SYMBOLIC',
      sp.simplify(Theta * fI - (fI.T).inv() * Theta - 2 * sp.I * Theta) == sp.zeros(k, k),
      'theta o f - (f*)^{-1} o theta = i theta + i theta = 2i theta for f = i id')
# D(f) = (f*)^{-1} is covariant on isomorphisms: ((g f)*)^{-1} = (g*)^{-1} (f*)^{-1}.
P_ = sp.Matrix(2, 2, sp.symbols('p0:4'))
Q_ = sp.Matrix(2, 2, sp.symbols('q0:4'))
check('thm:1:doubledual(D covariant)', 'SYMBOLIC',
      sp.simplify(((Q_ * P_).T).inv() - (Q_.T).inv() * (P_.T).inv()) == sp.zeros(2, 2),
      'V -> V*, f -> (f*)^{-1} is a covariant functor on isomorphisms')

# ex:1:basis -- the basis 2 e_i turns theta (e_i -> e^i) into theta / 4.  theta' sends
# e'_i = 2 e_i to e'^i = e^i / 2; its matrix in the original dual bases:
Bp = 2 * sp.eye(k)                                     # columns: the new basis in the old
dual_new = (Bp.inv()).T                                # columns: e'^i in the old dual basis
theta_new = dual_new * Bp.inv()                        # theta'(x) = sum_i x'^i e'^i
check('ex:1:basis', 'EXACT', theta_new == sp.eye(k) / 4,
      "with the basis 2 e_i the isomorphism e_i -> e^i becomes theta / 4")
theta_wrong = Bp.inv()                                 # forgets that the dual basis scales
control('ex:1:basis', 'EXACT', theta_wrong != sp.eye(k) / 4,
        "the model that rescales the basis but keeps e^i as the dual basis gives theta / 4")

# guard:1:riesz -- R(u) = <u, .> (antilinear in the first slot, Bible Q1) satisfies
# f* R(f u) = R(u) for unitary f, and R(i u) = -i R(u).
R = lambda u: (lambda w: (u.conj().T @ w)[0, 0])       # noqa: E731
Uq, _ = np.linalg.qr(crandn(3, 3))
u, w = crandn(3, 1), crandn(3, 1)
check('guard:1:riesz(unitary)', 'NUMERICAL', abs(R(Uq @ u)(Uq @ w) - R(u)(w)) < 1e-10,
      'f* R_W(f u) = R_V(u): <f u, f w> = <u, w> for unitary f')
check('guard:1:riesz(antilinear)', 'NUMERICAL', abs(R(1j * u)(w) - (-1j) * R(u)(w)) < 1e-10,
      'R(i u) = -i R(u)')
check('guard:1:riesz(f = i id)', 'NUMERICAL', abs(R(1j * u)(1j * w) - R(u)(w)) < 1e-10,
      'with f = i id: R(i u)(i w) = (-i)(i) R(u)(w) = R(u)(w), the test every linear theta fails')
Ainv = crandn(3, 3)
control('guard:1:riesz(invertible, not unitary)', 'NUMERICAL',
        abs(R(Ainv @ u)(Ainv @ w) - R(u)(w)) > 1e-6,
        'f* R(f u) = R(u) for an invertible f that is not unitary')

# thm:1:equiv -- M: Mat -> Vect_fd, with a chosen basis b_V for every V and G(f) = b_W^{-1} f b_V.
bV, bW, bX = crandn(3, 3), crandn(2, 2), crandn(4, 4)  # bases of V, W, X (invertible)
f_vw, g_wx = crandn(2, 3), crandn(4, 2)
Gm = lambda fm_, b_in, b_out: np.linalg.solve(b_out, fm_ @ b_in)   # noqa: E731
check('thm:1:equiv(G functor)', 'NUMERICAL',
      close(Gm(g_wx @ f_vw, bV, bX), Gm(g_wx, bW, bX) @ Gm(f_vw, bV, bW)),
      'G(g o f) = G(g) G(f): the bases cancel')
check('thm:1:equiv(natural)', 'NUMERICAL', close(f_vw @ bV, bW @ Gm(f_vw, bV, bW)),
      'f o b_V = b_W o MG(f): b is a natural isomorphism MG => id')
check('thm:1:equiv(GM = id)', 'NUMERICAL', close(Gm(f_vw, np.eye(3), np.eye(2)), f_vw),
      'with b = id on C^n, G(M(A)) = A')

# guard:1:isoequiv -- Mat is skeletal: a 2x3 A and a 3x2 B never give BA = I_3.
A_ = sp.Matrix(2, 3, sp.symbols('x0:6'))
B_ = sp.Matrix(3, 2, sp.symbols('y0:6'))
check('guard:1:isoequiv', 'SYMBOLIC', sp.expand((B_ * A_).det()) == 0,
      'det(BA) = 0 for every 3x2 B and 2x3 A, so m = n whenever m and n are isomorphic in Mat')
control('guard:1:isoequiv', 'SYMBOLIC', sp.expand((A_ * B_).det()) != 0,
        'det(AB) = 0 for the 2x2 product (it is not identically zero)')

print()
if FAILS:
    print(f'{len(FAILS)} FAILED: ' + ', '.join(FAILS))
    sys.exit(1)
print('All L1 checks pass.')
Lecture 2: check-l2-algebra.py 
"""Algebra script for Lecture 2, *Universal properties, adjunctions, semisimple categories*.

Representative finite-dimensional calculations that lectures/lec02.tex prints are checked
here before they are printed, on instances, each labelled SYMBOLIC (sympy, generic
entries), EXACT (integers, exact matrices) or NUMERICAL (floating point on random
instances), most with a control that must fail.  Universal claims rest on the lecture's
proofs and their review.
L2 prints no diagram equation; its pictures are commutative diagrams of universal
properties.

Conventions: as L1 (Bible K1; composition g o f, f first).  Tensor products of matrices
are Kronecker products with the first factor the slow index (Linear Algebra L12), so
u (x) v is np.kron(u, v).  Rep(S3): S3 acts on C^3 by permuting coordinates (L1's rho);
the standard representation is its restriction to the sum-zero plane, in Symmetries'
basis f1 = e1 - e2, f2 = e2 - e3 (lec04.tex:473-482); sgn is the sign.  Hom spaces of
representations are computed as null spaces of the intertwiner equations, exactly.

Run:  python3 scripts/check-l2-algebra.py     (exit status 1 on failure)
"""
import itertools
import sys

import numpy as np
import sympy as sp

FAILS = []
RNG = np.random.default_rng(20261022)


def check(label, kind, ok, what):
    print(f"{'OK  ' if ok else 'FAIL'} [{kind:9s}] {label}: {what}")
    if not ok:
        FAILS.append(label)


def control(label, kind, failed, what):
    check(label + ' (control)', kind, failed, what + ' -- must fail, and does')


def crandn(*shape):
    return RNG.standard_normal(shape) + 1j * RNG.standard_normal(shape)


def close(a, b, tol=1e-10):
    return np.allclose(a, b, atol=tol, rtol=0)


# ===================================================================== S2.1 universal properties
# ex:2:setvect -- in Set the product and the coproduct differ: |X x Y| = 6, |X u Y| = 5 for 2, 3.
X, Y = range(2), range(3)
prod = list(itertools.product(X, Y))
coprod = [(0, x) for x in X] + [(1, y) for y in Y]
check('ex:2:setvect(Set)', 'EXACT', (len(prod), len(coprod)) == (6, 5),
      'in Set, |X x Y| = 6 and |X u Y| = 5 for |X| = 2, |Y| = 3')

# def:2:biproduct -- C^2 (+) C^3 with its injections and projections.
m, n = 2, 3
i1 = np.vstack([np.eye(m), np.zeros((n, m))])
i2 = np.vstack([np.zeros((m, n)), np.eye(n)])
p1, p2 = i1.T, i2.T
check('def:2:biproduct', 'EXACT',
      close(p1 @ i1, np.eye(m)) and close(p2 @ i2, np.eye(n)) and close(p1 @ i2, 0)
      and close(p2 @ i1, 0) and close(i1 @ p1 + i2 @ p2, np.eye(m + n)),
      'p_k i_l = delta_kl id and i1 p1 + i2 p2 = id on C^2 (+) C^3')
control('def:2:biproduct', 'EXACT', not close(i1 @ p1, np.eye(m + n)),
        'i1 p1 = id alone (dropping the second summand)')

# eq:2:matrixarrows -- a map X1 (+) X2 -> Y1 (+) Y2 is the matrix (p_j f i_k); composing is
# the matrix product of these matrices of maps.
dims_x, dims_y, dims_z = (2, 3), (1, 2), (3, 1)


def blocks(dims):
    tot = sum(dims)
    inj, proj, off = [], [], 0
    for d in dims:
        I = np.zeros((tot, d))
        I[off:off + d, :] = np.eye(d)
        inj.append(I)
        proj.append(I.T)
        off += d
    return inj, proj


iX, pX = blocks(dims_x)
iY, pY = blocks(dims_y)
iZ, pZ = blocks(dims_z)
f = crandn(sum(dims_y), sum(dims_x))
g = crandn(sum(dims_z), sum(dims_y))
fjk = [[pY[j] @ f @ iX[k] for k in range(2)] for j in range(2)]
check('eq:2:matrixarrows(reassemble)', 'NUMERICAL',
      close(sum(iY[j] @ fjk[j][k] @ pX[k] for j in range(2) for k in range(2)), f),
      'f = sum_{j,k} i_j f_jk p_k')
gf = g @ f
ok = all(close(pZ[l] @ gf @ iX[k], sum((pZ[l] @ g @ iY[j]) @ fjk[j][k] for j in range(2)))
         for l in range(2) for k in range(2))
check('eq:2:matrixarrows(compose)', 'NUMERICAL', ok, '(g o f)_lk = sum_j g_lj o f_jk')
control('eq:2:matrixarrows(compose)', 'NUMERICAL',
        not close(pZ[0] @ gf @ iX[0], (pZ[0] @ g @ iY[0]) @ fjk[0][0]),
        '(g o f)_11 = g_11 o f_11, keeping only the j = 1 term of the sum')

# ===================================================================== S2.2 representables
# prop:2:repunique -- a natural isomorphism alpha: Hom(X, -) => Hom(X', -) is
# alpha_Y(f) = f o phi with phi = alpha_X(id_X), and phi is invertible.  Instance in Vect_fd
# with X = X' = C^3: alpha_Y(f) = f o phi for an invertible phi.
phi = crandn(3, 3)
alpha = lambda fY: fY @ phi                            # noqa: E731
fy, gyy = crandn(2, 3), crandn(4, 2)
check('prop:2:repunique(natural)', 'NUMERICAL', close(alpha(gyy @ fy), gyy @ alpha(fy)),
      "alpha_{Y'}(g o f) = g o alpha_Y(f): precomposition is natural in Y")
check('prop:2:repunique(phi)', 'NUMERICAL', close(alpha(np.eye(3)), phi) and close(alpha(fy), fy @ alpha(np.eye(3))),
      'phi = alpha_X(id_X), and alpha_Y(f) = f o phi')
psi = np.linalg.inv(phi)
alpha_inv = lambda gY: gY @ psi                        # noqa: E731
check('prop:2:repunique(inverse)', 'NUMERICAL',
      close(alpha_inv(np.eye(3)), psi) and close(phi @ psi, np.eye(3)) and close(psi @ phi, np.eye(3)),
      'psi = alpha^{-1}_{X\'}(id), and phi psi = id, psi phi = id')
g33 = crandn(3, 3)
post = lambda h: phi @ h                               # noqa: E731  postcomposition, X = Y = C^3
f33 = crandn(3, 3)
control('prop:2:repunique(natural)', 'NUMERICAL', not close(post(g33 @ f33), g33 @ post(f33)),
        'alpha_Y(f) = phi o f (postcomposing) is natural in Y')

# ===================================================================== S2.3 adjunctions
# thm:2:tensorhom -- Phi: Hom(U (x) V, W) -> Hom(U, Hom(V, W)), Phi(f)(u)(v) = f(u (x) v).
# Represent Phi(f) as the array T[w, u, v] = f[w, (u, v)] (u slow), i.e. a reshape.
dU, dV, dW = 2, 3, 2
fUVW = crandn(dW, dU * dV)


def Phi(fm, dU=dU, dV=dV):
    return fm.reshape(fm.shape[0], dU, dV)             # T[w, u, v]


def Phi_inv(T):
    return T.reshape(T.shape[0], -1)


u, v = crandn(dU), crandn(dV)
check('thm:2:tensorhom(Phi)', 'NUMERICAL',
      close(np.einsum('wuv,u,v->w', Phi(fUVW), u, v), fUVW @ np.kron(u, v)),
      'Phi(f)(u)(v) = f(u (x) v)')
# The inverse by the universal property: from g: U -> Hom(V, W) (here T[w, u, v]) form the
# bilinear map (u, v) -> g(u)(v) and the unique linear f with f(e_i (x) e_j) = g(e_i)(e_j).
def from_bilinear(T):
    fm = np.zeros((T.shape[0], dU * dV), dtype=complex)
    for i_ in range(dU):
        for j_ in range(dV):
            fm[:, i_ * dV + j_] = np.einsum('wuv,u,v->w', T, np.eye(dU)[i_], np.eye(dV)[j_])
    return fm


Tg = crandn(dW, dU, dV)                                # an arbitrary g
check('thm:2:tensorhom(bijection)', 'NUMERICAL',
      close(Phi(from_bilinear(Tg)), Tg) and close(from_bilinear(Phi(fUVW)), fUVW),
      'Theta and the universal-property inverse are mutually inverse (both directions)')
# naturality in U: a: U' -> U, Phi(f o (a (x) id_V)) = Phi(f) o a
dU2 = 4
a = crandn(dU, dU2)
lhs = Phi(fUVW @ np.kron(a, np.eye(dV)), dU=dU2)
rhs = np.einsum('wuv,ux->wxv', Phi(fUVW), a)
check('thm:2:tensorhom(natural in U)', 'NUMERICAL', close(lhs, rhs), 'Phi(f o (a (x) id)) = Phi(f) o a')
control('thm:2:tensorhom(natural in U)', 'NUMERICAL',
        not close(lhs, np.einsum('wuv,ux->wxv', Phi(fUVW), a.conj())),
        'Phi(f o (a (x) id)) = Phi(f) o conj(a)')
# naturality in W: b: W -> W', Phi(b o f) = (b o -) o Phi(f)
b = crandn(3, dW)
check('thm:2:tensorhom(natural in W)', 'NUMERICAL',
      close(Phi(b @ fUVW), np.einsum('xw,wuv->xuv', b, Phi(fUVW))), 'Phi(b o f) = Hom(V, b) o Phi(f)')

# unit and counit: unit_U(u) = (v -> u (x) v); counit_W(g (x) v) = g(v), and the triangle
# identities  counit_{U (x) V} o (unit_U (x) id_V) = id_{U (x) V},
#             Hom(V, counit_W) o unit_{Hom(V, W)} = id_{Hom(V, W)}.
# unit_U as a matrix C^{dU} -> Hom(V, U (x) V) = C^{(dU dV) x dV}, flattened row-major.
def unit_U(uvec):
    return np.kron(uvec.reshape(-1, 1), np.eye(dV))   # (dU dV) x dV matrix: v -> u (x) v


def counit_W(Gm, vvec):
    return Gm @ vvec                                    # g (x) v -> g(v)


uu, vv = crandn(dU), crandn(dV)
check('def:2:adjunction(triangle 1)', 'NUMERICAL', close(counit_W(unit_U(uu), vv), np.kron(uu, vv)),
      'counit(unit(u) (x) v) = u (x) v')
Gm = crandn(dW, dV)


def counit_tensor(t):
    """counit on Hom(V, W) (x) V, the tensor given as an array t[w, a, b] (G_{wa} v_b):
    sum_a t[w, a, a], i.e. g (x) v -> g(v)."""
    return np.einsum('waa->w', t)


ok = True
for e in np.eye(dV):
    t = np.einsum('wa,b->wab', Gm, e)                  # unit_{Hom(V,W)}(G)(e) = G (x) e
    ok &= close(counit_tensor(t), Gm @ e)
check('def:2:adjunction(triangle 2)', 'NUMERICAL', ok,
      'Hom(V, counit) o unit sends G to G: v -> counit(G (x) v) = G v, on a basis')
control('def:2:adjunction(triangle 2)', 'NUMERICAL',
        not close(np.einsum('wab->w', np.einsum('wa,b->wab', Gm, np.eye(dV)[0])), Gm @ np.eye(dV)[0]),
        'a counit summing all entries of G (x) v instead of the trace over the matching slots')

# ============================================================ S2.4 linear and semisimple
S3 = list(itertools.permutations(range(3)))


def rho3(s):
    M = sp.zeros(3, 3)
    for i in range(3):
        M[s[i], i] = 1
    return M


def sgn(s):
    return sp.Matrix([[sp.Matrix(rho3(s)).det()]])


F = sp.Matrix([[1, 0], [-1, 1], [0, -1]])               # f1 = e1 - e2, f2 = e2 - e3


def std(s):
    return (F.T * F).inv() * F.T * rho3(s) * F        # the action on span(f1, f2)


triv = lambda s: sp.Matrix([[1]])                      # noqa: E731
check('ex:2:s3(std is a representation)', 'EXACT',
      all(rho3(s) * F == F * std(s) for s in S3)
      and all(std(tuple(s[t[i]] for i in range(3))) == std(s) * std(t) for s in S3 for t in S3),
      'the sum-zero plane is invariant, and std is a homomorphism (f1, f2 basis)')


def homdim(A, B):
    """dim Hom_G(A, B): the null space of T A(g) = B(g) T for all g."""
    da, db = A(S3[0]).shape[0], B(S3[0]).shape[0]
    T = sp.Matrix(db, da, sp.symbols(f't0:{da * db}'))
    eqs = []
    for s in S3:
        eqs += list(T * A(s) - B(s) * T)
    Mx = sp.Matrix([[sp.diff(e, t) for t in T] for e in eqs])
    return da * db - Mx.rank()


irr = {'1': triv, 'sgn': sgn, 'std': std}
schur = all(homdim(irr[a_], irr[b_]) == (1 if a_ == b_ else 0) for a_ in irr for b_ in irr)
check('def:2:semisimple(S1) for Rep(S3)', 'EXACT', schur,
      'Hom(X_i, X_j) = C delta_ij for the three irreducibles of S3 (Schur)')
mult = {k: homdim(irr[k], rho3) for k in irr}
check('ex:2:s3(multiplicities)', 'EXACT', mult == {'1': 1, 'sgn': 0, 'std': 1},
      'm_i = dim Hom(X_i, C^3) = 1, 0, 1: C^3 = 1 (+) std')
check('ex:2:s3(End)', 'EXACT', homdim(rho3, rho3) == 2 == sum(k * k for k in mult.values()),
      'dim End(C^3) = 2 = 1^2 + 0^2 + 1^2: spanned by I and L1\'s u o s (the ones matrix J here)')
a_, b_ = sp.symbols('a b')
Jm = sp.ones(3, 3)
P1 = Jm / 3
Pstd = sp.eye(3) - Jm / 3
check('ex:2:s3(aI + bJ)', 'SYMBOLIC',
      sp.simplify(a_ * sp.eye(3) + b_ * Jm - ((a_ + 3 * b_) * P1 + a_ * Pstd)) == sp.zeros(3, 3)
      and P1 * P1 == P1 and Pstd * Pstd == Pstd and P1 * Pstd == sp.zeros(3, 3),
      'a I + b J = (a + 3b) P_1 + a P_std: the scalars (a + 3b, a) on the two simples')
control('ex:2:s3(aI + bJ)', 'SYMBOLIC',
        sp.simplify(a_ * sp.eye(3) + b_ * Jm - ((a_ + b_) * P1 + a_ * Pstd)) != sp.zeros(3, 3),
        'a I + b J = (a + b) P_1 + a P_std')


def regular(s):
    """The regular representation of S3 on C[S3]."""
    idx = {t: k for k, t in enumerate(S3)}
    M = sp.zeros(6, 6)
    for t in S3:
        M[idx[tuple(s[t[i]] for i in range(3))], idx[t]] = 1
    return M


mreg = {k: homdim(irr[k], regular) for k in irr}
check('ex:2:s3(regular)', 'EXACT', mreg == {'1': 1, 'sgn': 1, 'std': 2},
      'C[S3] = 1 (+) sgn (+) std^2')
check('thm:2:schur(Hom formula)', 'EXACT',
      homdim(rho3, regular) == sum(mult[k] * mreg[k] for k in irr) == 3,
      'dim Hom(C^3, C[S3]) = sum_i m_i(C^3) m_i(C[S3]) = 1*1 + 0*1 + 1*2 = 3')
control('thm:2:schur(Hom formula)', 'EXACT', homdim(rho3, regular) != sum(mult[k] + mreg[k] for k in irr),
        'dim Hom = sum_i (m_i(C^3) + m_i(C[S3])), sums instead of products')

# ex:2:end -- End(1 (+) 1 (+) std) = M_2(C) x C: dimension 5, not commutative.
def X112(s):
    M = sp.zeros(4, 4)
    M[0, 0] = M[1, 1] = 1
    M[2:4, 2:4] = std(s)
    return M


check('ex:2:end', 'EXACT', homdim(X112, X112) == 2 * 2 + 1 * 1 == 5,
      'dim End(1 (+) 1 (+) std) = 2^2 + 1^2 = 5')
E12 = sp.zeros(4, 4); E12[0, 1] = 1
E21 = sp.zeros(4, 4); E21[1, 0] = 1
check('ex:2:end(non-commutative)', 'EXACT',
      all(E12 * X112(t) == X112(t) * E12 and E21 * X112(t) == X112(t) * E21 for t in S3)
      and E12 * E21 != E21 * E12,
      'the matrix units E12, E21 between the two trivial summands are intertwiners and do not commute')
# thm:2:schur(inverse) -- the map zeta_X: G(H(X)) -> X of the proof, for X = C^3 in Rep(S3):
# basis vectors of H_1(X) = Hom(1, C^3) and H_std(X) = Hom(std, C^3) (random multiples of u
# and of the inclusion F of the plane) assemble to zeta_X = [c1 u | c2 F]; it must be an
# intertwiner from 1 (+) std to C^3, and invertible.
c1, c2 = sp.Rational(3, 7), sp.Rational(-5, 2)
uvec = sp.Matrix([1, 1, 1])
zeta = sp.Matrix.hstack(c1 * uvec, c2 * F)
plus = lambda s_: sp.diag(triv(s_), std(s_))             # noqa: E731  1 (+) std
check('thm:2:schur(inverse)', 'EXACT', all(zeta * plus(t) == rho3(t) * zeta for t in S3) and zeta.det() != 0,
      'zeta_X = (b_ij) : 1 (+) std -> C^3 is an invertible intertwiner, for fixed non-zero basis scalars')
# naturality f o zeta_X = zeta_X o G(H(f)) for f = a I + b (u o s) in End(C^3): in the bases
# (c1 u) of H_1 and (c2 F e_1, c2 F e_2) of H_std, G(H(f)) = diag(a + 3b, a, a).
fa, fb = sp.symbols('f_a f_b')
fmat = fa * sp.eye(3) + fb * sp.ones(3, 3)
GHf = sp.diag(fa + 3 * fb, fa, fa)
check('thm:2:schur(inverse, natural)', 'SYMBOLIC', sp.simplify(fmat * zeta - zeta * GHf) == sp.zeros(3, 3),
      'f o zeta_X = zeta_X o G(H(f)) with G(H(f)) = diag(a + 3b, a, a)')
control('thm:2:schur(inverse, natural)', 'SYMBOLIC',
        sp.simplify(fmat * zeta - zeta * sp.diag(fa + fb, fa, fa)) != sp.zeros(3, 3),
        'G(H(f)) = diag(a + b, a, a) (the wrong scalar on the trivial summand)')
bad_zeta = sp.Matrix.hstack(sp.Matrix([1, 0, 0]), c2 * F)
control('thm:2:schur(inverse)', 'EXACT', any(bad_zeta * plus(t) != rho3(t) * bad_zeta for t in S3),
        'a "basis vector" e1 that is not a morphism 1 -> C^3 still assembles to an intertwiner')

# rem:2:z3 -- Rep(Z3) also has three simple objects (three characters, Z3 abelian), so
# Rep(S3), Rep(Z3) and Vect_fd^3 are equivalent as linear categories.
w = sp.exp(2 * sp.pi * sp.I / 3)
chars = [lambda k, j=j: sp.Matrix([[w ** (j * k)]]) for j in range(3)]
Z3 = list(range(3))


def homdimZ3(A, B):
    T = sp.Symbol('t')
    eqs = [sp.simplify(T * A(k)[0, 0] - B(k)[0, 0] * T) for k in Z3]
    return 0 if any(sp.simplify(e) != 0 for e in eqs) else 1


check('rem:2:z3', 'EXACT', all(homdimZ3(chars[i], chars[j]) == (1 if i == j else 0)
                                for i in range(3) for j in range(3))
      and all(sp.simplify(chars[j](k1 + k2)[0, 0] - chars[j](k1)[0, 0] * chars[j](k2)[0, 0]) == 0
              for j in range(3) for k1 in Z3 for k2 in Z3),
      'Z3 has three one-dimensional irreducibles k -> w^{jk}, pairwise non-isomorphic')
# rem:2:dims -- dim X = sum_i m_i(X) dim X_i with dims 1, 1, 2 for S3:
check('rem:2:dims', 'EXACT', 1 * 1 + 0 * 1 + 1 * 2 == 3 and 1 * 1 + 1 * 1 + 2 * 2 == 6,
      'dim C^3 = 1*1 + 0*1 + 1*2 = 3 and dim C[S3] = 1 + 1 + 2*2 = 6')
control('rem:2:dims', 'EXACT', 1 + 1 + 2 != 6, 'dim C[S3] = sum of multiplicities 1 + 1 + 2')

# ex:2:currying -- in Set, functions X x B -> C are functions X -> C^B: |C|^{|X||B|}.
Xs, Bs, Cs = 2, 3, 2
check('ex:2:currying', 'EXACT', Cs ** (Xs * Bs) == (Cs ** Bs) ** Xs,
      '|Hom(X x B, C)| = |C|^{|X||B|} = |Hom(X, C^B)|')

# ============================================================ S2.5 a Jordan block
N = sp.Matrix([[0, 1], [0, 0]])
rhoZ = lambda k: sp.eye(2) + k * N                     # noqa: E731
kk, ll = sp.symbols('k l', integer=True)
check('ex:2:jordan(representation)', 'SYMBOLIC', sp.simplify(rhoZ(kk + ll) - rhoZ(kk) * rhoZ(ll)) == sp.zeros(2, 2),
      'rho(k + l) = rho(k) rho(l) for rho(k) = [[1, k], [0, 1]]')
Tz = sp.Matrix(2, 2, sp.symbols('z0:4'))
solZ = sp.solve(list(Tz * rhoZ(1) - rhoZ(1) * Tz), list(Tz), dict=True)[0]
EndZ = Tz.subs(solZ)
check('guard:2:jordan(End)', 'SYMBOLIC', len(EndZ.free_symbols) == 2 and N * rhoZ(1) == rhoZ(1) * N,
      'End(C^2) = span(I, N) is two-dimensional, so C^2 is not one of the X_i (S1)')
ev = rhoZ(1).eigenvects()
check('guard:2:jordan', 'EXACT', len(ev) == 1 and ev[0][1] == 2 and len(ev[0][2]) == 1
      and ev[0][2][0].normalized() == sp.Matrix([1, 0]),
      'rho(1) has the single eigenvalue 1 with a single eigenline C e1: no invariant complement')
control('guard:2:jordan', 'EXACT', len((sp.eye(2) + 0 * N).eigenvects()[0][2]) != 1,
        'with N replaced by 0 (a semisimple representation) there is still only one eigenline')

print()
if FAILS:
    print(f'{len(FAILS)} FAILED: ' + ', '.join(FAILS))
    sys.exit(1)
print('All L2 checks pass.')
Lecture 3: check-l3-algebra.py 
"""Algebra script for Lecture 3, *Monoidal categories and string diagrams*.

Every formula, number and diagram equation that lectures/lec03.tex prints is checked here
before it is printed, on instances, labelled SYMBOLIC, EXACT or NUMERICAL, most with a
control that must fail.  A string-diagram equation is checked as an equation between
composites of Kronecker products of matrices: time runs upward (a box drawn above another
acts after it), and the left strand is the first (slow) Kronecker factor (Bible K1).  The
diagram equations of L3 are exact equalities, with no scalar.

Conventions (Bible K20): the associator runs (X (x) Y) (x) Z -> X (x) (Y (x) Z); the unitors
are l_X: 1 (x) X -> X and r_X: X (x) 1 -> X.  In skeletal Vec_G^omega the associator of
simple objects is alpha_{g,h,k} = omega(g, h, k) id, and a monoidal functor that is the
identity on objects has J_{g,h} = j(g, h) id and J_0 = j0 id.

Run:  python3 scripts/check-l3-algebra.py     (exit status 1 on failure)
"""
import itertools
import sys

import numpy as np
import sympy as sp

FAILS = []
RNG = np.random.default_rng(20261023)


def check(label, kind, ok, what):
    print(f"{'OK  ' if ok else 'FAIL'} [{kind:9s}] {label}: {what}")
    if not ok:
        FAILS.append(label)


def control(label, kind, failed, what):
    check(label + ' (control)', kind, failed, what + ' -- must fail, and does')


def crandn(*shape):
    return RNG.standard_normal(shape) + 1j * RNG.standard_normal(shape)


def close(a, b, tol=1e-10):
    return np.allclose(a, b, atol=tol, rtol=0)


K = np.kron

# ================================================================ S3.1 the definition
# ex:3:mat -- Mat_C with the Kronecker product is strict: (A (x) B) (x) C = A (x) (B (x) C)
# exactly, and the 1x1 matrix 1 is a unit on both sides.
A, B, C = crandn(2, 3), crandn(3, 2), crandn(2, 2)
check('ex:3:mat(associative)', 'NUMERICAL', close(K(K(A, B), C), K(A, K(B, C))),
      'Kronecker: (A (x) B) (x) C = A (x) (B (x) C) as matrices')
check('ex:3:mat(unit)', 'NUMERICAL', close(K(np.eye(1), A), A) and close(K(A, np.eye(1)), A),
      '1 (x) A = A = A (x) 1 for the 1 x 1 identity')
control('ex:3:mat(unit)', 'NUMERICAL', not close(K(2 * np.eye(1), A), A),
        'the 1 x 1 matrix (2) is a unit for the Kronecker product')

# ex:3:vect -- in Vect the associator (u (x) v) (x) w -> u (x) (v (x) w) is natural:
# alpha o ((f (x) g) (x) h) = (f (x) (g (x) h)) o alpha.  In coordinates (slow index first)
# both triple products are the same column, so alpha is the identity matrix.
u, v, w = crandn(2), crandn(3), crandn(2)
check('ex:3:vect(alpha)', 'NUMERICAL', close(K(K(u, v), w), K(u, K(v, w))),
      'in coordinates (slow index first) alpha is the identity matrix: Kronecker associativity')

# ex:3:rep -- Rep(G): g acts on V (x) W by rho_V(g) (x) rho_W(g), a homomorphism.
S3 = list(itertools.permutations(range(3)))


def rho(s):
    M = np.zeros((3, 3))
    for i in range(3):
        M[s[i], i] = 1
    return M


def comp(s, t):
    return tuple(s[t[i]] for i in range(3))


check('ex:3:rep', 'EXACT', all(close(K(rho(comp(s, t)), rho(comp(s, t))),
                                     K(rho(s), rho(s)) @ K(rho(t), rho(t))) for s in S3 for t in S3),
      'g -> rho(g) (x) rho(g) is a representation on C^3 (x) C^3 (interchange)')
control('ex:3:rep', 'EXACT', not all(close(K(rho(comp(s, t)), rho(comp(s, t))),
                                           K(rho(t), rho(t)) @ K(rho(s), rho(s))) for s in S3 for t in S3),
        'g -> rho(g) (x) rho(g) reversing the order of multiplication')

# ================================================================ S3.3 interchange
f, g = crandn(3, 2), crandn(2, 3)
h, k = crandn(2, 2), crandn(4, 2)
check('thm:3:interchange', 'NUMERICAL', close(K(g, k) @ K(f, h), K(g @ f, k @ h)),
      '(g (x) k) o (f (x) h) = (g o f) (x) (k o h)')
control('thm:3:interchange', 'NUMERICAL', not close(K(g, k) @ K(f, h), K(k @ h, g @ f)),
        'the factors exchanged on one side')
# eq:3:slide -- (id (x) h) o (f (x) id) = f (x) h = (f (x) id) o (id (x) h)
fs, hs = crandn(3, 2), crandn(4, 5)
one_two = K(np.eye(3), hs) @ K(fs, np.eye(5))
two_one = K(fs, np.eye(4)) @ K(np.eye(2), hs)
check('eq:3:slide', 'NUMERICAL', close(one_two, K(fs, hs)) and close(two_one, K(fs, hs)),
      'boxes on different strands slide past each other: all three pictures agree')
control('eq:3:slide', 'NUMERICAL', not close(K(fs, hs), K(hs, fs)),
        'f (x) h = h (x) f (the strands exchanged)')

# ================================================================ S3.4 Eckmann-Hilton
# thm:3:scalars -- in a strict monoidal category s o t = s (x) t = t (x) s = t o s for
# s, t in End(1).  In Vect, End(C) = C and (x) of 1x1 matrices is their product.
s1, t1 = crandn(1, 1), crandn(1, 1)
check('thm:3:scalars', 'NUMERICAL',
      close(s1 @ t1, K(s1, t1)) and close(K(s1, t1), K(t1, s1)) and close(K(t1, s1), t1 @ s1),
      's o t = s (x) t = t (x) s = t o s for scalars')
check('thm:3:scalars(interchange steps)', 'NUMERICAL',
      close(K(s1, np.eye(1)) @ K(np.eye(1), t1), K(s1, t1))
      and close(K(np.eye(1), t1) @ K(s1, np.eye(1)), K(s1, t1)),
      '(s (x) id) o (id (x) t) = s (x) t = (id (x) t) o (s (x) id), with 1 (x) 1 = 1')
# eq:3:floating -- a scalar box beside f: s (x) f = s f, and it may float to any height
fv = crandn(3, 2)
# the printed equation: the scalar box beside f, below it and above it (same side):
# (f (x) id_1) o (id_X (x) s) = (id_Y (x) s) o (f (x) id_1), with 1 = C (1 x 1)
fsq = crandn(2, 3)
check('eq:3:floating', 'NUMERICAL',
      close(K(fsq, np.eye(1)) @ K(np.eye(3), s1), K(np.eye(2), s1) @ K(fsq, np.eye(1))),
      'the scalar box slides up beside f on the same side (interchange)')
# control: in a strict monoidal category whose tensor of scalar arrows is twisted by
# complex conjugation on the strand X (f (x) h = f sigma_a(h), sigma_X = conjugation; the
# model of C.3-L03 round 2), s (x) id_X and id_X (x) s differ: the scalar cannot cross X.
lam = 1j
control('eq:3:floating(cross the strand)', 'NUMERICAL', not np.isclose(lam, np.conj(lam)),
        'in the conjugation-twisted model, s (x) id_X = lambda equals id_X (x) s = conj(lambda)')
check('eq:3:floating(Vect)', 'NUMERICAL', close(K(s1, fv), s1[0, 0] * fv) and close(K(fv, s1), s1[0, 0] * fv),
      'in Vect, with bilinear tensor, s (x) f = f (x) s = s f: the scalar floats anywhere')
# non-commuting End(X) for X != 1, as a control: End(C^2) is not commutative
a2, b2 = crandn(2, 2), crandn(2, 2)
control('thm:3:scalars', 'NUMERICAL', not close(a2 @ b2, b2 @ a2), 'End(X) is commutative for X = C^2')

# ================================================================ S3.5 Vec_G^omega
def pentagon_ok(omega, G, mul):
    """The pentagon for alpha_{g,h,k} = omega(g,h,k) id, on every quadruple: the two paths
    (W(x)X)(x)Y)(x)Z -> W(x)(X(x)(Y(x)Z)) multiply out to
        omega(gh,k,m) omega(g,h,km)   and   omega(g,h,k) omega(g,hk,m) omega(h,k,m)."""
    bad = []
    for g_, h_, k_, m_ in itertools.product(G, repeat=4):
        two = omega(mul(g_, h_), k_, m_) * omega(g_, h_, mul(k_, m_))
        three = omega(g_, h_, k_) * omega(g_, mul(h_, k_), m_) * omega(h_, k_, m_)
        if sp.simplify(two - three) != 0:
            bad.append((g_, h_, k_, m_))
    return bad


# thm:3:vecgw(Z2) -- normalised omega on Z2 = {0, 1} (1 = s): only omega(s,s,s) = eps is free
eps = sp.Symbol('epsilon')
Z2 = [0, 1]
mul2 = lambda a, b: (a + b) % 2                          # noqa: E731
om = lambda g_, h_, k_: eps if (g_, h_, k_) == (1, 1, 1) else 1  # noqa: E731
eqs = set()
for g_, h_, k_, m_ in itertools.product(Z2, repeat=4):
    two = om(mul2(g_, h_), k_, m_) * om(g_, h_, mul2(k_, m_))
    three = om(g_, h_, k_) * om(g_, mul2(h_, k_), m_) * om(h_, k_, m_)
    e = sp.simplify(two - three)
    if e != 0:
        eqs.add(sp.factor(e))
check('thm:3:vecgw(Z2 equations)', 'SYMBOLIC', eqs == {sp.factor(1 - eps ** 2)} or eqs == {sp.factor(eps ** 2 - 1)},
      'of the 16 pentagons only (s,s,s,s) is not automatic, and it reads 1 = eps^2')
check('thm:3:vecgw(Z2 solutions)', 'SYMBOLIC', set(sp.solve(1 - eps ** 2, eps)) == {1, -1},
      'eps = omega(s,s,s) = +1 or -1')
check('thm:3:vecgw(Z2 instance)', 'EXACT',
      sp.sympify(om(1, 1, 1)).subs(eps, -1) ** 2 == 1
      and pentagon_ok(lambda a, b, c: sp.sympify(om(a, b, c)).subs(eps, -1), Z2, mul2) == [],
      'omega(s,s,s) = -1 passes all 16 pentagons')
control('thm:3:vecgw(Z2 instance)', 'EXACT',
        pentagon_ok(lambda a, b, c: sp.sympify(om(a, b, c)).subs(eps, sp.I), Z2, mul2) != [],
        'omega(s,s,s) = i passes the pentagons')


# ex:3:z2(normalisation) -- h = 1 in the pentagon, using the triangle,
# gives w(g,k,m) = w(g,k,m) w(1,k,m).  Here 0 is the group identity.
# Keep w(1,k,m) free so this verifies the substitution, not just a pre-normalised table.
wfree = {t: sp.Symbol('v' + ''.join(map(str, t)), nonzero=True)
         for t in itertools.product(Z2, repeat=3)}
wnorm = lambda a, b, c: sp.Integer(1) if b == 0 else wfree[(a, b, c)]
normalisation_steps = []
for a, c, d in itertools.product(Z2, repeat=3):
    two = wnorm(mul2(a, 0), c, d) * wnorm(a, 0, mul2(c, d))
    three = wnorm(a, 0, c) * wnorm(a, c, d) * wnorm(0, c, d)
    normalisation_steps.append(sp.simplify(two - three
                               - wnorm(a, c, d) * (1 - wnorm(0, c, d))) == 0)
# With first-slot normalisation now established, k = 1 leaves only w(g,h,1).
wnorm_first = lambda a, b, c: sp.Integer(1) if a == 0 or b == 0 else wfree[(a, b, c)]
for a, b, d in itertools.product(Z2, repeat=3):
    two = wnorm_first(mul2(a, b), 0, d) * wnorm_first(a, b, d)
    three = wnorm_first(a, b, 0) * wnorm_first(a, b, d) * wnorm_first(b, 0, d)
    normalisation_steps.append(sp.simplify(two - three
                               - wnorm_first(a, b, d) * (1 - wnorm_first(a, b, 0))) == 0)
check('ex:3:z2(normalisation substitution)', 'SYMBOLIC', all(normalisation_steps),
      'h = 1 gives w(g,k,m) = w(g,k,m) w(1,k,m); nonzero factors cancel')
control('ex:3:z2(normalisation substitution)', 'EXACT',
        pentagon_ok(lambda a, b, c: 2 if (a, b, c) == (0, 1, 1) else 1, Z2, mul2) != [],
        'the triangle alone permits w(1,s,s) = 2, but the pentagon does not')


# ex:3:zn -- EGNO (2.34): omega_p(a,b,c) = exp(2 pi i p a (b + c - [b+c]_N) / N^2) is a
# normalised 3-cocycle on Z_N; check N = 2, 3, 4 and every p.
def omega_p(N, p):
    return lambda a, b, c: sp.exp(2 * sp.pi * sp.I * p * a * (b + c - (b + c) % N) / N ** 2)


ok = True
for N in (2, 3, 4):
    ZN = list(range(N))
    mulN = lambda a, b, N=N: (a + b) % N               # noqa: E731
    for p in range(N):
        w_ = omega_p(N, p)
        ok &= pentagon_ok(w_, ZN, mulN) == []
        ok &= all(sp.simplify(w_(a, 0, c) - 1) == 0 for a in ZN for c in ZN)
check('ex:3:zn', 'SYMBOLIC', ok, 'omega_p is a normalised 3-cocycle on Z_N for N = 2, 3, 4, all p')
check('ex:3:zn(Z2)', 'SYMBOLIC', sp.simplify(omega_p(2, 1)(1, 1, 1) + 1) == 0,
      'for N = 2, p = 1: omega(s,s,s) = exp(2 pi i * 2 / 4) = -1')
bad = lambda a, b, c: sp.exp(2 * sp.pi * sp.I * a * (b + c) / 9)  # noqa: E731
control('ex:3:zn', 'SYMBOLIC', pentagon_ok(bad, list(range(3)), lambda a, b: (a + b) % 3) != [],
        'without the carry term -[b+c]_N (N = 3, p = 1) omega is a cocycle')
triv = lambda a, b, c: sp.exp(2 * sp.pi * sp.I * a * (b + c - (b + c) % 3) / 3)  # noqa: E731
check('ex:3:zn(N not N^2)', 'SYMBOLIC', all(sp.simplify(triv(a, b, c) - 1) == 0 for a in range(3) for b in range(3) for c in range(3)),
      'with N in place of N^2 the formula is identically 1: the N^2 matters')

# ex:3:s3z3 -- the tensor product separates Rep(S3) from Rep(Z3): std (x) std = 1 (+) sgn (+) std,
# while the characters of Z3 multiply, chi_i (x) chi_j = chi_{i+j}.
Fm = sp.Matrix([[1, 0], [-1, 1], [0, -1]])


def rho3(s_):
    M = sp.zeros(3, 3)
    for i in range(3):
        M[s_[i], i] = 1
    return M


def std(s_):
    return (Fm.T * Fm).inv() * Fm.T * rho3(s_) * Fm


def sgn(s_):
    return sp.Matrix([[rho3(s_).det()]])


def triv(s_):
    return sp.Matrix([[1]])


def kron(Am, Bm):
    return sp.kronecker_product(Am, Bm)


def homdim(Ar, Br):
    da, db = Ar(S3[0]).shape[0], Br(S3[0]).shape[0]
    T = sp.Matrix(db, da, sp.symbols(f't0:{da * db}'))
    eqs = []
    for s_ in S3:
        eqs += list(T * Ar(s_) - Br(s_) * T)
    Mx = sp.Matrix([[sp.diff(e, t) for t in T] for e in eqs])
    return da * db - Mx.rank()


stst = lambda s_: kron(std(s_), std(s_))                # noqa: E731
mults = [homdim(irr, stst) for irr in (triv, sgn, std)]
check('ex:3:s3z3(std x std)', 'EXACT', mults == [1, 1, 1] and 4 == 1 + 1 + 2,
      'std (x) std = 1 (+) sgn (+) std: multiplicities 1, 1, 1 and dimensions 4 = 1 + 1 + 2')
w3 = sp.exp(2 * sp.pi * sp.I / 3)
check('ex:3:s3z3(Z3)', 'SYMBOLIC', all(sp.simplify(w3 ** (i * k) * w3 ** (j * k) - w3 ** (((i + j) % 3) * k)) == 0
                                       for i in range(3) for j in range(3) for k in range(3)),
      'chi_i (x) chi_j = chi_{i+j} for the characters chi_j(k) = w^{jk} of Z3')
control('ex:3:s3z3(std x std)', 'EXACT', homdim(stst, stst) != 1,
        'std (x) std is simple (End = C), as for a tensor product of characters')

# eq:3:vectpentagon -- in Vect both paths send ((w (x) x) (x) y) (x) z to w (x) (x (x) (y (x) z)):
# in coordinates every bracketing is the same Kronecker column.
w4, x4, y4, z4 = crandn(2), crandn(2), crandn(3), crandn(2)
check('eq:3:vectpentagon', 'NUMERICAL', close(K(K(K(w4, x4), y4), z4), K(w4, K(x4, K(y4, z4)))),
      'in coordinates every bracketing is the same Kronecker column, so both pentagon paths agree')

# rem:3:coboundary -- the coherence omega' j(h,k) j(g,hk) = j(g,h) j(gh,k) omega says
# omega' = omega (delta j)^{-1} with (delta j)(g,h,k) = j(h,k) j(g,hk) / (j(gh,k) j(g,h)) (Bible K20);
# and if omega, omega' are normalised, the condition at (g,1,h) gives j(g,1) = j(1,h).
jgen = {(a, b): sp.Symbol(f'J{a}{b}', nonzero=True) for a in Z2 for b in Z2}
dj = lambda a, b, c: jgen[(b, c)] * jgen[(a, mul2(b, c))] / (jgen[(mul2(a, b), c)] * jgen[(a, b)])  # noqa: E731
wsym = {t: sp.Symbol('w' + ''.join(map(str, t)), nonzero=True) for t in itertools.product(Z2, repeat=3)}
ok = True
for t in itertools.product(Z2, repeat=3):
    a, b, c = t
    wprime = sp.solve(sp.Eq(sp.Symbol('wp') * jgen[(b, c)] * jgen[(a, mul2(b, c))],
                            jgen[(a, b)] * jgen[(mul2(a, b), c)] * wsym[t]), sp.Symbol('wp'))[0]
    ok &= sp.simplify(wprime - wsym[t] / dj(a, b, c)) == 0
check('rem:3:coboundary', 'SYMBOLIC', ok, "the coherence gives omega' = omega (delta j)^{-1}, delta j as in K20")
control('rem:3:coboundary', 'SYMBOLIC',
        any(sp.simplify(sp.solve(sp.Eq(sp.Symbol('wp') * jgen[(b, c)] * jgen[(a, mul2(b, c))],
                                       jgen[(a, b)] * jgen[(mul2(a, b), c)] * wsym[(a, b, c)]), sp.Symbol('wp'))[0]
                        - wsym[(a, b, c)] * dj(a, b, c)) != 0 for a, b, c in itertools.product(Z2, repeat=3)),
        "omega' = omega (delta j), the opposite sign convention")
step = sp.solve(sp.Eq(1 * jgen[(0, 1)] * jgen[(1, 1)], jgen[(1, 0)] * jgen[(1, 1)] * 1), jgen[(1, 0)])
check('rem:3:coboundary(normalised)', 'SYMBOLIC', step == [jgen[(0, 1)]],
      'normalised omega, omega\': the condition at (s,1,s) gives j(s,1) = j(1,s)')

# ================================================================ S3.6 monoidal functors
# thm:3:vecgw(inequivalent) -- an identity-on-objects monoidal functor Vec_Z2^omega ->
# Vec_Z2^omega' with J_{g,h} = j(g,h) id, J_0 = j0 id: the unit axioms (trivial unitors,
# omega normalised) force j(1,g) = j(g,1) = 1/j0, and the coherence (Bible K20)
#   omega'(g,h,k) j(h,k) j(g,hk) = j(g,h) j(gh,k) omega(g,h,k)
# at (s,s,s) then reads omega'(s,s,s) = omega(s,s,s).
j0, jss, ep, epp = sp.symbols('j0 j_ss epsilon epsilon_p')
jf = lambda a, b: 1 / j0 if 0 in (a, b) else jss        # noqa: E731
lhs = epp * jf(1, 1) * jf(1, mul2(1, 1))
rhs = jf(1, 1) * jf(mul2(1, 1), 1) * ep
check('thm:3:vecgw(inequivalent)', 'SYMBOLIC', sp.solve(sp.Eq(lhs, rhs), epp) == [ep],
      "at (s,s,s) the coherence gives omega'(s,s,s) = omega(s,s,s): eps is invariant")
# the coherence at every triple, for the normalised j: consistent with omega' = omega
ok = all(sp.simplify(om(a, b, c) * jf(b, c) * jf(a, mul2(b, c)) - jf(a, b) * jf(mul2(a, b), c) * om(a, b, c)) == 0
         for a, b, c in itertools.product(Z2, repeat=3))
check('thm:3:vecgw(identity functor)', 'SYMBOLIC', ok,
      "with omega' = omega every normalised j satisfies the coherence (any j_ss, j0)")

# prop:3:z2 (normalised coboundaries) -- if omega' = omega * delta j is normalised whenever
# omega is, then j(g,1) = j(1,h) for all g, h, and the ratio at (s,s,s) is 1.
ja = {(a, b): sp.Symbol(f'j{a}{b}') for a in Z2 for b in Z2}
ratio = lambda a, b, c: ja[(a, b)] * ja[(mul2(a, b), c)] / (ja[(b, c)] * ja[(a, mul2(b, c))])  # noqa: E731
norm_eqs = [sp.numer(sp.together(ratio(a, 0, c) - 1)) for a in Z2 for c in Z2]
sol = sp.solve(norm_eqs, [ja[(1, 0)], ja[(0, 1)]], dict=True)
check('prop:3:z2(normalised coboundary)', 'SYMBOLIC',
      all(sp.simplify(ratio(1, 1, 1).subs(sd) - 1) == 0 for sd in sol) and len(sol) > 0,
      'a coboundary that keeps omega normalised has ratio 1 at (s,s,s)')
control('prop:3:z2(normalised coboundary)', 'SYMBOLIC', sp.simplify(ratio(1, 1, 1) - 1) != 0,
        'an arbitrary coboundary (j(s,1), j(1,s) free) has ratio 1 at (s,s,s)')

print()
if FAILS:
    print(f'{len(FAILS)} FAILED: ' + ', '.join(FAILS))
    sys.exit(1)
print('All L3 checks pass.')
Lecture 4: check-l4-algebra.py 
"""Algebra script for Lecture 4, *Duality: cups, caps and quantum dimension*.

Every formula, number and diagram equation that lectures/lec04.tex prints is checked here
before it is printed, on instances, labelled SYMBOLIC, EXACT or NUMERICAL, most with a
control that must fail.  A string diagram is evaluated as a composite of Kronecker
products, read upward with the left strand the first (slow) factor (Bible K1).  Duals as
Bible K3: ev_X : X* (x) X -> 1 (a cap, X* on the left), coev_X : 1 -> X (x) X* (a cup, X
on the left); with a pivotal structure a, the other cap is
    ev~_X = ev_{X*} o (a_X (x) id_{X*}) : X (x) X* -> 1
and the other cup coev~_X : 1 -> X* (x) X.  In Vect_fd, a is L1's canonical map, and in
coordinates ev~ and coev~ are again the identity matrix reshaped.  The diagram equations
of L4 are exact equalities unless a scalar is stated.

Spin-1/2 (Bible K4): Omega(e1, e2) = 1 = -Omega(e2, e1); as a cup C -> V (x) V it is
e1 (x) e2 - e2 (x) e1.

Run:  python3 scripts/check-l4-algebra.py     (exit status 1 on failure)
"""
import itertools
import sys

import numpy as np
import sympy as sp

FAILS = []
RNG = np.random.default_rng(20261024)


def check(label, kind, ok, what):
    print(f"{'OK  ' if ok else 'FAIL'} [{kind:9s}] {label}: {what}")
    if not ok:
        FAILS.append(label)


def control(label, kind, failed, what):
    check(label + ' (control)', kind, failed, what + ' -- must fail, and does')


def crandn(*shape):
    return RNG.standard_normal(shape) + 1j * RNG.standard_normal(shape)


def close(a, b, tol=1e-10):
    return np.allclose(a, b, atol=tol, rtol=0)


def K(*ms):
    out = np.eye(1)
    for m in ms:
        out = np.kron(out, m)
    return out


# ================================================================ S4.1 duals and the snake
n = 3
I = np.eye(n)
coev = I.reshape(n * n, 1)                       # 1 -> X (x) X*:   sum_i e_i (x) e^i
ev = I.reshape(1, n * n)                         # X* (x) X -> 1:   e^i (x) e_j -> delta_ij
check('eq:4:snakes(1)', 'NUMERICAL', close(K(I, ev) @ K(coev, I), I),
      '(id_X (x) ev_X)(coev_X (x) id_X) = id_X')
check('eq:4:snakes(2)', 'NUMERICAL', close(K(ev, I) @ K(I, coev), I),
      '(ev_X (x) id_X*)(id_X* (x) coev_X) = id_X*')
control('eq:4:snakes(1)', 'NUMERICAL', not close(K(I, ev) @ K(2 * coev, I), I),
        'the snake with coev scaled by 2 is still the identity')

# thm:4:snake(a) -- two left duals (X*, ev, coev) and (X', ev', coev') with X' = X* via an
# invertible P (ev' = ev o (P^{-1} (x) id), coev' = (id (x) P) coev): phi = (ev (x) id_X')
# (id_X* (x) coev') : X* -> X' is P, and is the unique compatible isomorphism.
P = crandn(n, n)
evp = ev @ K(np.linalg.inv(P), I)
coevp = K(I, P) @ coev
phi = K(ev, I) @ K(I, coevp)
check('thm:4:snake(unique)', 'NUMERICAL', close(phi, P),
      'phi = (ev (x) id)(id (x) coev\') : X* -> X\' recovers the comparison P')
check('thm:4:snake(compatible)', 'NUMERICAL',
      close(evp @ K(phi, I), ev) and close(K(I, phi) @ coev, coevp),
      "ev' o (phi (x) id) = ev and (id (x) phi) o coev = coev'")
check('thm:4:snake(primed snakes)', 'NUMERICAL', close(K(I, evp) @ K(coevp, I), I),
      'the primed pair satisfies the snake too')

# eq:4:rotated -- f* = (ev_Y (x) id_X*)(id_Y* (x) f (x) id_X*)(id_Y* (x) coev_X) is the
# transpose (L1's dual map), and (g o f)* = f* o g*.
m = 2
Iy = np.eye(m)
evY = Iy.reshape(1, m * m)
f = crandn(m, n)                                 # f : X = C^3 -> Y = C^2
fstar = K(evY, I) @ K(Iy, f, I) @ K(Iy, coev)
check('eq:4:rotated', 'NUMERICAL', close(fstar, f.T), 'the rotated box is the transpose: f* = f^T')
control('eq:4:rotated', 'NUMERICAL', not close(fstar, f.conj().T), 'the rotated box is the adjoint f^dagger')
g = crandn(4, m)
gstar = g.T
check('thm:4:snake(contravariant)', 'NUMERICAL', close((g @ f).T, fstar @ gstar), '(g o f)* = f* o g*')

# ex:4:rep -- the contragredient rho*(g) = (rho(g)^{-1})^T makes ev and coev intertwiners.
S3 = list(itertools.permutations(range(3)))


def rho(s):
    M = np.zeros((3, 3))
    for i in range(3):
        M[s[i], i] = 1
    return M


Rg = lambda s: K(rho(s), np.eye(1))                     # noqa: E731
ok_ev = all(close(ev @ K(np.linalg.inv(rho(s)).T, rho(s)), ev) for s in S3)
ok_coev = all(close(K(rho(s), np.linalg.inv(rho(s)).T) @ coev, coev) for s in S3)
# a non-unitary representation, to see the inverse-transpose (Z acting by [[1,1],[0,1]])
A = np.array([[1.0, 1.0], [0.0, 1.0]])
ev2, coev2 = np.eye(2).reshape(1, 4), np.eye(2).reshape(4, 1)
ok_jordan = close(ev2 @ K(np.linalg.inv(A).T, A), ev2) and close(K(A, np.linalg.inv(A).T) @ coev2, coev2)
check('ex:4:rep', 'NUMERICAL', ok_ev and ok_coev and ok_jordan,
      'ev and coev intertwine rho* (x) rho and rho (x) rho* with the trivial representation')
control('ex:4:rep', 'NUMERICAL', not close(ev2 @ K(A, A), ev2),
        'with rho*(g) = rho(g) (not inverse-transposed) ev is an intertwiner')

# ex:4:vecg -- Vec_G^omega with identity unitors and coev = id: the first snake is
# r o (id (x) ev) o alpha_{g,g^-1,g} o (coev (x) id) o l^{-1} = ev * omega(g, g^-1, g), so
# ev_g = omega(g, g^-1, g)^{-1}; the second snake then holds by the cocycle condition.
def omega_p(N, p):
    return lambda a, b, c: sp.exp(2 * sp.pi * sp.I * p * a * (b + c - (b + c) % N) / N ** 2)


ok = True
for N, p in [(2, 1), (3, 1), (4, 1), (4, 3)]:
    w = omega_p(N, p)
    for gg in range(N):
        ginv = (-gg) % N
        ev_g = 1 / w(gg, ginv, gg)
        snake1 = ev_g * w(gg, ginv, gg)
        snake2 = ev_g / w(ginv, gg, ginv)              # alpha^{-1}_{g^-1, g, g^-1}
        ok &= sp.simplify(snake1 - 1) == 0 and sp.simplify(snake2 - 1) == 0
check('ex:4:vecg', 'SYMBOLIC', ok,
      'ev_g = omega(g,g^-1,g)^{-1}, coev_g = 1 satisfy both snakes (omega_p, several N, p)')
w2 = omega_p(2, 1)
check('ex:4:vecg(Z2)', 'SYMBOLIC', sp.simplify(w2(1, 1, 1) + 1) == 0,
      'for Z2 with omega(s,s,s) = -1 the naive snake (ev = coev = id) is -id_s')
control('ex:4:vecg', 'SYMBOLIC', sp.simplify(sp.exp(2 * sp.pi * sp.I / 3) * omega_p(3, 1)(1, 2, 1) - 1) != 0,
        'ev_g = omega(g,g^-1,g) (not its inverse) satisfies the first snake for Z3')

# prop:4:tensordual -- (X (x) Y)* = Y* (x) X* with the nested cup and cap:
# coev_{X (x) Y} = (id_X (x) coev_Y (x) id_X*) o coev_X : 1 -> X Y Y* X*,
# ev_{X (x) Y}  = ev_Y o (id_Y* (x) ev_X (x) id_Y) : Y* X* X Y -> 1; both snakes hold.
nx, ny = 2, 3
Ix, Iy3 = np.eye(nx), np.eye(ny)
coevX, evX = Ix.reshape(nx * nx, 1), Ix.reshape(1, nx * nx)
coevY, evY3 = Iy3.reshape(ny * ny, 1), Iy3.reshape(1, ny * ny)
coevXY = K(Ix, coevY, Ix) @ coevX
evXY = evY3 @ K(Iy3, evX, Iy3)
Ixy = np.eye(nx * ny)
check('prop:4:tensordual', 'NUMERICAL',
      close(K(Ixy, evXY) @ K(coevXY, Ixy), Ixy) and close(K(evXY, Ixy) @ K(Ixy, coevXY), Ixy),
      'the nested cup and cap make Y* (x) X* a left dual of X (x) Y (both snakes)')
coev_wrong = K(coevX, coevY)                              # X X* Y Y*: not nested
control('prop:4:tensordual', 'NUMERICAL', not close(K(Ixy, evXY) @ K(coev_wrong, Ixy), Ixy),
        'the unnested cup coev_X (x) coev_Y with the nested cap gives a straight snake')

# ================================================================ S4.2 bending wires
dU, dV, dW = 2, 3, 2
IU, IV, IW = np.eye(dU), np.eye(dV), np.eye(dW)
coevV, evV = IV.reshape(dV * dV, 1), IV.reshape(1, dV * dV)
fb = crandn(dW, dU * dV)                                # f : U (x) V -> W
bend = K(fb, IV) @ K(IU, coevV)                         # U -> W (x) V*
unbend = lambda gm: K(IW, evV) @ K(gm, IV)              # noqa: E731  W (x) V* (x) V -> W
check('thm:4:bend', 'NUMERICAL', close(unbend(bend), fb),
      'f -> (f (x) id)(id (x) coev) and g -> (id (x) ev)(g (x) id) are inverse')
gb = crandn(dW * dV, dU)
check('thm:4:bend(other order)', 'NUMERICAL', close(K(unbend(gb), IV) @ K(IU, coevV), gb),
      'and in the other order')
control('thm:4:bend', 'NUMERICAL', not close(unbend(K(fb, IV) @ K(IU, 2 * coevV)), fb),
        'bending with a cup scaled by 2 and unbending still gives f')

# teleportation: |psi> (x) Phi+ projected on Phi+ in the first two factors gives psi / 2.
psi = crandn(2)
phip = np.eye(2).reshape(4) / np.sqrt(2)
out = K(phip.reshape(1, 4).conj(), np.eye(2)) @ np.kron(psi, phip)
check('ex:4:teleport', 'NUMERICAL', close(out, psi / 2),
      '(<Phi+| (x) id)(|psi> (x) |Phi+>) = |psi>/2: the snake, with the normalisation 1/2')
X_ = np.array([[0, 1], [1, 0]])
phix = K(np.eye(2), X_) @ phip                           # (id (x) X) Phi+
outx = K(phix.reshape(1, 4).conj(), np.eye(2)) @ np.kron(psi, phip)
check('ex:4:teleport(Pauli)', 'NUMERICAL', close(X_ @ outx, psi / 2),
      'the outcome (id (x) X)Phi+ leaves X psi / 2, undone by X')
Z_ = np.diag([1, -1])
ok = True
for P_ in (Z_, X_ @ Z_):                                  # the other two outcomes (id (x) P) Phi+
    phiP = K(np.eye(2), P_) @ phip
    outP = K(phiP.reshape(1, 4).conj(), np.eye(2)) @ np.kron(psi, phip)
    ok &= close(np.linalg.inv(P_) @ outP, psi / 2)
check('ex:4:teleport(all four)', 'NUMERICAL', ok, 'the Z and XZ outcomes are undone by a Pauli matrix too')

# ================================================================ S4.3 pivotal, trace, dimension
# In Vect_fd with the canonical a: ev~ and coev~ are the identity reshaped (coordinates).
evt = I.reshape(1, n * n)                               # X (x) X* -> 1
coevt = I.reshape(n * n, 1)                             # 1 -> X* (x) X
h = crandn(n, n)
trL = (evt @ K(h, I) @ coev).item()                     # close to the right
trR = (ev @ K(I, h) @ coevt).item()                     # close to the left
check('def:4:trace', 'NUMERICAL', np.isclose(trL, np.trace(h)) and np.isclose(trR, np.trace(h)),
      'in Vect_fd both traces are the ordinary trace')
control('def:4:trace', 'NUMERICAL', not np.isclose(trL, np.trace(h @ h)), 'the left trace is tr(h^2)')
# cyclicity and multiplicativity
f1, g1 = crandn(n, m), crandn(m, n)
check('thm:4:pivotal(cyclic)', 'NUMERICAL', np.isclose(np.trace(f1 @ g1), np.trace(g1 @ f1)),
      'tr(f o g) = tr(g o f) for f: Y -> X, g: X -> Y')
h2 = crandn(m, m)
check('thm:4:pivotal(multiplicative)', 'NUMERICAL', np.isclose(np.trace(np.kron(h, h2)), np.trace(h) * np.trace(h2)),
      'tr(h (x) h\') = tr(h) tr(h\')')
check('thm:4:pivotal(dimension)', 'NUMERICAL', np.isclose(np.trace(np.kron(I, Iy)), n * m),
      'd_{X (x) Y} = d_X d_Y = dim X dim Y in Vect_fd')
# the partial trace: close the right strand of h12 on X (x) Y
h12 = crandn(n * m, n * m)
ptr = sum(K(I, Iy[:, [k]].T) @ h12 @ K(I, Iy[:, [k]]) for k in range(m))
ptr_pic = K(I, Iy.reshape(1, m * m)) @ K(h12, Iy) @ K(I, Iy.reshape(m * m, 1))
check('eq:4:partialtrace', 'NUMERICAL', close(ptr, ptr_pic),
      'closing the right strand is the partial trace over the second factor')
control('eq:4:partialtrace', 'NUMERICAL', not close(ptr_pic, K(I, Iy[:, [0]].T) @ h12 @ K(I, Iy[:, [0]])),
        'the partial trace keeps only the k = 1 term (one diagonal block)')

# a pivotal structure on Vec_Z2 with a_s = -1: d_s = ev~ coev with ev~ = ev_{s*} (a_s (x) id) and
# ev = coev = 1 (omega = 1), so d_s = a_s; d_1 = 1, and d_{s (x) s} = d_s^2 = 1 = d_1
a_piv = {0: 1, 1: -1}
d = {g_: 1 * (a_piv[g_] * 1) * 1 for g_ in (0, 1)}
check('ex:4:negdim', 'EXACT', d[1] == -1 and d[(1 + 1) % 2] == d[1] * d[1] and d[0] == 1,
      'Vec_Z2 with a_s = -1: d_s = -1, and d_{s (x) s} = d_s d_s = 1 = d_1')

# ex:4:z3pivot -- on Vec_Z3 a character chi: k -> w^k (w = e^{2 pi i/3}) gives a pivotal
# structure with d_g = chi(g); d_{g*} = chi(g^{-1}) = conj(chi(g)) != chi(g) for g = 1: not spherical.
w3 = sp.exp(2 * sp.pi * sp.I / 3)
dch = lambda k: w3 ** (k % 3)                             # noqa: E731
check('ex:4:z3pivot', 'SYMBOLIC',
      all(sp.simplify(dch((a_ + b_) % 3) - dch(a_) * dch(b_)) == 0 for a_ in range(3) for b_ in range(3))
      and sp.simplify(dch(1) - dch(2)) != 0 and sp.simplify(dch(2) - sp.conjugate(dch(1))) == 0,
      'd_g = chi(g) is multiplicative; d_{g*} = d_{g^-1} = conj(d_g) differs from d_g for g = 1')
# ex:4:repdims -- in Rep(S3) the loop on std is its dimension 2 (canonical pivotal structure)
Fm = np.array([[1, 0], [-1, 1], [0, -1]], dtype=float)
std = lambda s_: np.linalg.solve(Fm.T @ Fm, Fm.T @ rho(s_) @ Fm)  # noqa: E731
I2s = np.eye(2)
check('ex:4:repdims', 'NUMERICAL', np.isclose((I2s.reshape(1, 4) @ I2s.reshape(4, 1)).item(), 2)
      and all(np.isclose(np.trace(std(s_)), np.trace(rho(s_)) - 1) for s_ in S3),
      'the loop on std is 2 = dim std; its character is chi_perm - 1')

# ================================================================ S4.4 Penrose and Frobenius-Schur
I2 = np.eye(2)
Om = np.array([[0, 1], [-1, 0]]).reshape(4, 1)          # e1 (x) e2 - e2 (x) e1
OmD = Om.conj().T
check('thm:4:penrose(cap = cup dagger)', 'NUMERICAL',
      close(K(I2, OmD) @ K(Om, I2), -I2) and np.isclose((OmD @ Om).item(), 2),
      'with cap = Omega^dagger: snake = -id, loop = +2')
check('thm:4:penrose(cap = -cup dagger)', 'NUMERICAL',
      close(K(I2, -OmD) @ K(Om, I2), I2) and np.isclose((-OmD @ Om).item(), -2),
      'with cap = -Omega^dagger: snake = +id, loop = -2')
# the second snake for the same pair
check('thm:4:penrose(other snake)', 'NUMERICAL', close(K(-OmD, I2) @ K(I2, Om), I2),
      'the other snake with cap = -Omega^dagger is also +id')
# uniqueness: any cap c with (id (x) c)(Omega (x) id) = id is -Omega^dagger
cs = sp.Matrix(1, 4, sp.symbols('c0:4'))
OmS = sp.Matrix([0, 1, -1, 0])
snake = sp.kronecker_product(sp.eye(2), cs) * sp.kronecker_product(OmS, sp.eye(2))
sol = sp.solve(list(snake - sp.eye(2)), list(cs), dict=True)
check('thm:4:penrose(unique cap)', 'SYMBOLIC', len(sol) == 1 and sp.Matrix(1, 4, [sol[0][c] for c in cs]) == -OmS.T,
      'the snake equation forces the cap to be -Omega^dagger')
# invariance: U (x) U Omega = det(U) Omega, so Omega is SU(2)-invariant
U = np.linalg.qr(crandn(2, 2))[0]
U = U / np.sqrt(np.linalg.det(U))
check('ex:4:singlet(invariant)', 'NUMERICAL', close(K(U, U) @ Om, Om), '(U (x) U) Omega = Omega for U in SU(2)')
# Frobenius-Schur: j: V -> V* the map v -> Omega(., v), matrix J; j* o a = J^T = -J: kappa = -1
J = np.array([[0, 1], [-1, 0]])
check('def:4:fs(spin 1/2)', 'EXACT', close(J.T, -J), 'j* o a = J^T = -J: kappa_{1/2} = -1')
Sym = np.array([[1, 0], [0, 1]])
check('def:4:fs(symmetric)', 'EXACT', close(Sym.T, Sym), 'a symmetric invariant form gives kappa = +1')
control('def:4:fs(spin 1/2)', 'EXACT', not close(J.T, J), 'the singlet form is symmetric')
# Symmetries' character formula for S3's standard representation: (1/6) sum chi(g^2) = 1
chi_std = lambda s: np.trace(rho(s)) - 1                 # noqa: E731
comp = lambda s, t: tuple(s[t[i]] for i in range(3))     # noqa: E731
fs_std = sum(chi_std(comp(s, s)) for s in S3) / 6
check('def:4:fs(S3 std)', 'EXACT', np.isclose(fs_std, 1), 'Symmetries\' (1/|G|) sum chi(g^2) = 1 for S3\'s std')

# ================================================================ S4.5 dagger
# Hilb_fd with orthonormal bases: ev~ = coev^dagger and ev = coev~^dagger (dagger pivotal)
check('thm:4:dagger', 'NUMERICAL', close(evt, coev.conj().T) and close(ev, coevt.conj().T),
      'in Hilb_fd (orthonormal bases) the caps are the daggers of the cups')

# ================================================================ round-1 repairs (C.3 L4)
# thm:4:pivotal needs End(1) = C: Opus's strict model, objects Z2, End(g) = C x C, the generator
# acting on End(1) by the swap; s (x) id_g != id_g (x) s, and multiplicativity fails.
def _act(g_, r):
    return r if g_ == 0 else (r[1], r[0])


def _mul(r, t):
    return (r[0] * t[0], r[1] * t[1])


def _tens(g_, r, h_, t, act=_act):                         # (r on g) (x) (t on h)
    return _mul(r, act(g_, t))


one2 = (1, 1)
trL2 = lambda g_, f_, act=_act: _tens(g_, f_, g_, one2, act)   # noqa: E731  ev = coev = a = 1
s_ = (1, 0)
control('thm:4:pivotal(End 1 = C)', 'EXACT', trL2(1, _tens(1, one2, 0, s_)) != _mul(trL2(1, one2), trL2(0, s_)),
        'with End(1) = C x C (swap action) tr_L(id_g (x) s) = tr_L(id_g) tr_L(s)')
# the scalars that are numbers, (x, x), are fixed by the swap by definition, so they move out: this
# records the model's consistency (it is not a test that can fail for End(1) = C)
check('thm:4:pivotal(End 1 = C: consistency)', 'EXACT',
      all(trL2(g_, _tens(g_, one2, 0, (x_, x_))) == _mul(trL2(g_, one2), trL2(0, (x_, x_)))
          for g_ in (0, 1) for x_ in (2, -3, 5)),
      'for scalars x id_1 (End(1) = C) tr_L(id_g (x) x) = tr_L(id_g) tr_L(x)')

# thm:4:penrose and the pivotal structure: in the presentation X* = V, coev = Omega, ev = c = -Omega^dag
# (straight snakes), the canonical a: X -> X** is the matrix A with A^T C = C^T; d = ev~ coev with
# ev~ = c (a (x) id).  Canonical a gives d = +2 and kappa = -1; (-1)^{2j} a gives d = -2 and kappa = +1.
Om4 = np.array([0, 1, -1, 0.]).reshape(4, 1)
c4 = -Om4.T
C4 = c4.reshape(2, 2)
A4 = (C4.T @ np.linalg.inv(C4)).T
d_can = (c4 @ K(A4, np.eye(2)) @ Om4).item()
d_flip = (c4 @ K(-A4, np.eye(2)) @ Om4).item()
check('thm:4:penrose(pivotal)', 'NUMERICAL', close(A4, -np.eye(2)) and np.isclose(d_can, 2) and np.isclose(d_flip, -2)
      and np.isclose((c4 @ Om4).item(), -2),
      'canonical a = -id here, d_V = +2; the straight cap closes the loop with -a: -2')
# kappa: j = id_V : X -> X* (= V).  Its dual j*: X** -> X* is the rotated box of L4 (b), computed:
# j* = (ev_{X*} (x) id_{X*}) (id_{X**} (x) j (x) id_{X*}) (id_{X**} (x) coev_X), with ev_{X*} = c4 (the
# presentation X** = V uses the same pairing) and coev_X = Om4; then j* a = kappa j
I2_ = np.eye(2)
J4 = I2_
jstar = K(c4, I2_) @ K(I2_, J4, I2_) @ K(I2_, Om4)
kap_can = (jstar @ A4)[0, 0] / J4[0, 0]
kap_flip = (jstar @ (-A4))[0, 0] / J4[0, 0]
check('def:4:fs(pivotal)', 'NUMERICAL', close(jstar @ A4, kap_can * J4) and np.isclose(kap_can, -1) and np.isclose(kap_flip, 1),
      'j* computed as a rotated box; j* a = kappa j with kappa_{1/2} = -1 (canonical a), +1 ((-1)^{2j} a)')
control('thm:4:penrose(pivotal)', 'NUMERICAL', not np.isclose(d_can, -2), 'with the canonical a the loop is -2')
# eq:4:loop orientation on Vec_Z3 = {1, r, r^2} with a_g = chi(g): tr_L(id_r) = chi(r), tr_R(id_r) = chi(r)^{-1}
trL_r = 1 * w3 * 1                                         # ev_{r*} (a_r (x) id) coev_r
trR_r = 1 * (1 / w3) * 1                                   # ev_r (id (x) a_r^{-1}) coev~
# (these are the definitions evaluated on scalars; the record is that the two closures differ)
check('eq:4:loop(orientation, by definition)', 'SYMBOLIC', sp.simplify(trL_r - w3) == 0 and sp.simplify(trR_r - trL_r) != 0,
      'the clockwise and counter-clockwise loops on r differ (chi(r) vs chi(r)^-1): orientation matters')

# Writing revision: expand the first dual comparison using exact, non-orthogonal data.
# Different dual bases ensure that omitting phi, or its inverse, is detected.
Ix_exact = sp.eye(2)
co_exact = sp.Matrix([1, 0, 0, 1])
ev_exact = co_exact.T
P_exact = sp.Matrix([[2, 1], [1, 1]])
kp = sp.kronecker_product
ev_prime = ev_exact * kp(P_exact.inv(), Ix_exact)
co_prime = kp(Ix_exact, P_exact) * co_exact
phi_exact = kp(ev_exact, Ix_exact) * kp(Ix_exact, co_prime)
psi_exact = kp(ev_prime, Ix_exact) * kp(Ix_exact, co_exact)
snake_prime = kp(Ix_exact, ev_prime) * kp(co_prime, Ix_exact)
compat_steps = [ev_prime * kp(phi_exact, Ix_exact),
                ev_exact * kp(Ix_exact, kp(Ix_exact, ev_prime))
                * kp(Ix_exact, co_prime, Ix_exact),
                ev_exact * kp(Ix_exact, snake_prime), ev_exact]
check('thm:4:snake(expanded cancellation)', 'EXACT',
      all(a == b for a, b in zip(compat_steps, compat_steps[1:]))
      and psi_exact * phi_exact == kp(ev_exact, Ix_exact) * kp(Ix_exact, co_exact) == Ix_exact,
      "ev' (phi (x) id) = ev (id (x) primed snake) = ev; psi phi = the unprimed snake")
control('thm:4:snake(expanded cancellation)', 'EXACT',
        ev_prime * kp(Ix_exact, Ix_exact) != ev_exact and psi_exact != Ix_exact,
        'replace the comparison phi by id despite the nontrivial change of dual basis')

# The closing cap depends linearly on the pivotal structure, with the left-dual data fixed.
Om_exact = sp.Matrix([0, 1, -1, 0])
c_exact = -Om_exact.T
A_exact = -Ix_exact   # canonical pivotal map in the presentation V* = V, coev = Omega
closing_canonical = c_exact * kp(A_exact, Ix_exact)
closing_flipped = c_exact * kp(-A_exact, Ix_exact)
check('thm:4:penrose(closing cap)', 'EXACT',
      closing_canonical == Om_exact.T and closing_flipped == -Om_exact.T
      and closing_canonical * Om_exact == sp.Matrix([[2]])
      and closing_flipped * Om_exact == sp.Matrix([[-2]]),
      'canonical closing cap is Omega dagger; flipping a changes it to -Omega dagger')
control('thm:4:penrose(closing cap)', 'EXACT', closing_flipped != closing_canonical,
        'the closing cap stays fixed when the pivotal structure changes sign')

# Double transposition T(j) = j* a is an involution.  Verify the displayed chain
# with arbitrary j in canonical dual bases; no complex conjugation belongs in the dual.
J_exact = sp.Matrix(2, 2, sp.symbols('j0:4'))
fs_steps = [(J_exact.T * Ix_exact).T * Ix_exact,
            Ix_exact.T * J_exact.T.T * Ix_exact,
            Ix_exact.T * Ix_exact * J_exact, J_exact]
check('def:4:fs(dual again)', 'SYMBOLIC',
      all(a == b for a, b in zip(fs_steps, fs_steps[1:])),
      '(j* a)* a = a* j** a = a* a_X* j = j, using naturality and pivotal compatibility')
lam_exact = 2 + sp.I
j_skew = sp.Matrix([[0, 1], [-1, 0]])
check('def:4:fs(scale independent)', 'EXACT', (lam_exact * j_skew).T == -lam_exact * j_skew,
      'dual is linear: rescaling a self-duality does not change kappa')
control('def:4:fs(scale independent)', 'EXACT',
        (lam_exact * j_skew).conjugate().T != -lam_exact * j_skew,
        'use the antilinear adjoint instead of the dual in the FS relation')
check('eq:4:flags(operational)', 'EXACT',
      kp(Ix_exact, Om_exact.T) * kp(Om_exact, Ix_exact) == -Ix_exact
      and kp(Ix_exact, -Om_exact.T) * kp(Om_exact, Ix_exact) == Ix_exact,
      'same-way flags are Omega and Omega dagger; reversing the cap gives a straight snake')
control('eq:4:flags(operational)', 'EXACT',
        kp(Ix_exact, Om_exact.T) * kp(Om_exact, Ix_exact) != Ix_exact,
        'same-way flags already give a straight snake')

print()
if FAILS:
    print(f'{len(FAILS)} FAILED: ' + ', '.join(FAILS))
    sys.exit(1)
print('All L4 checks pass.')
Lecture 5: check-l5-algebra.py 
"""Algebra script for Lecture 5, *Fusion categories: particles and how they fuse*.

This script checks, on instances, the fusion rules, ring identities, FP dimensions, vertex
normalisations and fusion-space counts that lectures/lec05.tex prints, labelled SYMBOLIC,
EXACT or NUMERICAL, most with a control that must fail.  The diagram equations are checked
as matrix identities in Rep(S3) (the bubble and theta there hold by the construction of the
vertices; completeness and the cup are genuine tests); pictures, flags and orientations are
checked by rendering, not here.  Not checked here: the cited results (unitary =>
pseudo-unitary, the canonical spherical structure, D8/Q8) and the imported pivotal example
Vec_Z2 with a_s = -id (checked in check-l4-algebra.py, ex:4:negdim).  Fusion rules are given as N[a][b][c] = N_{ab}^c (Bible K5 symbol
table); duals by a -> a*.  Vertices use the isotopy-invariant normalisation of Bible K5:
    bubble        psi^{ab dag}_c psi^{ab}_{c'} = delta_{cc'} sqrt(d_a d_b / d_c) id_c,
    completeness  id_a (x) id_b = sum_c sqrt(d_c / (d_a d_b)) psi^{ab}_c psi^{ab dag}_c,
checked in Rep(S3), where vertices are scaled isometric intertwiners.

Run:  python3 scripts/check-l5-algebra.py     (exit status 1 on failure)
"""
import itertools
import sys

import numpy as np
import sympy as sp

FAILS = []


def check(label, kind, ok, what):
    print(f"{'OK  ' if ok else 'FAIL'} [{kind:9s}] {label}: {what}")
    if not ok:
        FAILS.append(label)


def control(label, kind, failed, what):
    check(label + ' (control)', kind, failed, what + ' -- must fail, and does')


def close(a, b, tol=1e-9):
    return np.allclose(a, b, atol=tol, rtol=0)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------- the fusion rings
phi = (1 + sp.sqrt(5)) / 2


def ring_fib():
    L = ['1', 't']
    N = {a: {b: {c: 0 for c in L} for b in L} for a in L}
    for a in L:
        N['1'][a][a] = N[a]['1'][a] = 1
    N['t']['t']['1'] = N['t']['t']['t'] = 1
    return L, N, {'1': '1', 't': 't'}


def ring_ising():
    L = ['1', 's', 'p']
    N = {a: {b: {c: 0 for c in L} for b in L} for a in L}
    for a in L:
        N['1'][a][a] = N[a]['1'][a] = 1
    N['s']['s']['1'] = N['s']['s']['p'] = 1
    N['s']['p']['s'] = N['p']['s']['s'] = 1
    N['p']['p']['1'] = 1
    return L, N, {a: a for a in L}


def ring_su2(k):
    L = [sp.Rational(n, 2) for n in range(k + 1)]
    N = {a: {b: {c: 0 for c in L} for b in L} for a in L}
    for a, b in itertools.product(L, repeat=2):
        j = abs(a - b)
        while j <= min(a + b, k - a - b):
            N[a][b][j] = 1
            j += 1
    return L, N, {a: a for a in L}


def ring_group(G, mul, inv):
    N = {a: {b: {c: int(mul(a, b) == c) for c in G} for b in G} for a in G}
    return list(G), N, {g: inv(g) for g in G}


def ring_s3():
    L = ['1', 'sgn', 'std']
    N = {a: {b: {c: 0 for c in L} for b in L} for a in L}
    for a in L:
        N['1'][a][a] = N[a]['1'][a] = 1
    N['sgn']['sgn']['1'] = 1
    N['sgn']['std']['std'] = N['std']['sgn']['std'] = 1
    N['std']['std']['1'] = N['std']['std']['sgn'] = N['std']['std']['std'] = 1
    return L, N, {a: a for a in L}


def associative(L, N):
    for a, b, c, e in itertools.product(L, repeat=4):
        lhs = sum(N[a][b][x] * N[x][c][e] for x in L)
        rhs = sum(N[b][c][y] * N[a][y][e] for y in L)
        if lhs != rhs:
            return False
    return True


def rigidity(L, N, dual):
    unit = L[0]
    ok = all(N[a][b][c] == N[b][dual[c]][dual[a]] for a, b, c in itertools.product(L, repeat=3))
